using SMARTtools.Core.StructuralDTO;
using System.Collections.Generic;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Table
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents a single row within a table, containing its cells
    /// and row-level compliance / semantic metadata.
    /// </summary>
    public class TableRowDTO
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Zero-based index of the row within the table.
        /// </summary>
        public int RowIndex { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Cells contained in this row.
        /// </summary>
        public List<TableCellDTO> Cells { get; set; } = new List<TableCellDTO>();

        // --------------------------------------------------------------------
        // COMPLIANCE
        // --------------------------------------------------------------------

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether this row has compliance issues
        /// (e.g., inconsistent formatting, unreadable text).
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasComplianceIssues { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Optional compliance summary for row-level issues.
        /// </summary>
        public string ComplianceSummary { get; set; }

        // --------------------------------------------------------------------
        // SEMANTIC / ANALYTICS
        // --------------------------------------------------------------------

        /// <summary>
        /// Optional semantic category (e.g., "HeaderRow", "DataRow").
        /// </summary>
        public string SemanticCategory { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Optional analytics score indicating clarity or importance.
        /// </summary>
        public double? AnalyticsScore { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Optional free-form analytics notes.
        /// </summary>
        public string AnalyticsNotes { get; set; }
    }
}

// SMARTtools.Core.DTO
// TableStyleDTO.cs
//
// PURPOSE:
//   Represents theme-based styling applied to a table, including:
//   - Header row presence
//   - Total row presence
//   - Banded rows/columns
//   - Theme style identifier
//   This DTO allows SMARTcomply and SMARTmanage to evaluate
//   consistency, readability, and brand compliance.
//
// CONTRACT NOTES:
//   - Stable DTO contract: do not remove or rename existing properties.
//   - ThemeStyleId corresponds to PowerPoint's built-in table style IDs.

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Table
{
    /// <summary>
    /// DTO describing theme-based styling applied to a table.
    /// </summary>
    public class TableStyleDTO
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the table includes a header row.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasHeaderRow { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the table includes a total row.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasTotalRow { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the table uses banded rows.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasBandedRows { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the table uses banded columns.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasBandedColumns { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// PowerPoint theme style identifier for the table.
        /// </summary>
        public string ThemeStyleId { get; set; }
    }
}

// SMARTtools.Core.DTO
// AudioTransformDTO.cs
//
// PURPOSE:
//   Represents geometric transformations applied to an audio icon on a slide,
//   including rotation, flipping, and z-order stacking. Although audio is not
//   a visual media object, its icon behaves like any other shape in PowerPoint.
//
//   These values influence layout, compliance, broadcast sequencing,
//   and semantic interpretation.
//
// CONTRACT NOTES:
//   - Stable DTO contract: do not remove or rename existing properties.
//   - Rotation is expressed in degrees (0–360).
//   - Z-order follows PowerPoint's stacking index (higher = closer to front).
//   - Flipping flags indicate horizontal/vertical mirroring.

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Transforms
{
    /// <summary>
    /// DTO describing geometric transformations applied to an audio icon.
    /// </summary>
    public class AudioTransformDTO
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Rotation angle in degrees (0–360).
        /// </summary>
        public double Rotation { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the audio icon is flipped horizontally.
        /// </summary>
        public bool FlipHorizontal { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Indicates whether the audio icon is flipped vertically.
        /// </summary>
        public bool FlipVertical { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Z-order stacking position (higher = closer to front).
        /// </summary>
        public int ZOrder { get; set; }
    }
}

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// AnimationTypeENUM provides a simplified, high?level classification of
    /// PowerPoint animation types. SMARTtools does not mirror the full
    /// PowerPoint animation taxonomy — instead, it normalizes animations into
    /// core categories used across geometry, diagnostics, and broadcast rules.
    ///
    /// Consumed by:
    /// - AnimationGeometryBuilder (motion path + timing normalization)
    /// - AnimationDTO (animation metadata container)
    /// - IssueDetector (animation compliance rules)
    /// - SMARTshare / SMARTcast (broadcast animation behavior)
    /// - SMARTmanage (analytics + governance)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - Stable contract: values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - This enum represents the *type* of animation, not the specific effect.
    /// - Specific effects (Fade, Fly In, Wipe, etc.) are handled separately.
    /// </summary>
    public enum AnimationTypeENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// No animation applied.
        /// </summary>
        None = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Entrance animation (object appears).
        /// Examples: Fade In, Fly In, Zoom In.
        /// </summary>
        Entrance = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Exit animation (object disappears).
        /// Examples: Fade Out, Fly Out, Zoom Out.
        /// </summary>
        Exit = 2,

        /// <summary>
        /// Emphasis animation (object changes while remaining visible).
        /// Examples: Pulse, Spin, Grow/Shrink.
        /// </summary>
        Emphasis = 3,

        /// <summary>
        /// Motion path animation (object moves along a defined path).
        /// Examples: Line, Arc, Custom Path.
        /// </summary>
        MotionPath = 4,

        /// <summary>
        /// Unknown or unclassified animation type.
        /// Used when PowerPoint exposes an unsupported or legacy animation.
        /// </summary>
        Unknown = 100
    }
}

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// FontWeightENUM defines the normalized weight classification used by
    /// SMARTtools when extracting and analyzing text from PowerPoint shapes.
    ///
    /// PowerPoint exposes many font weight variations (Light, Medium,
    /// SemiBold, ExtraBold, etc.), but SMARTtools simplifies these into
    /// core categories for consistency across:
    ///
    /// - TextDTO (text metadata container)
    /// - TextStyleDTO (style normalization)
    /// - GeometryBuilder (text layout + bounding box adjustments)
    /// - IssueDetector (font-weight compliance rules)
    /// - SMARTshare / SMARTcast (broadcast text rendering)
    /// - SMARTmanage (analytics + governance)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - Stable contract: values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - Represents normalized font weight, not the raw PowerPoint weight.
    /// </summary>
    public enum FontWeightENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Standard font weight (e.g., Regular).
        /// </summary>
        Normal = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Slightly heavier than normal (e.g., SemiBold, Medium).
        /// </summary>
        SemiBold = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Fully bold text (e.g., Bold, ExtraBold).
        /// </summary>
        Bold = 2
    }
}

using System;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ObjectTypeENUM defines the high-level classification of any object
    /// extracted from a PowerPoint slide. This is the FIRST identity layer used by:
    ///
    /// - IdentityDTO (object identity metadata)
    /// - ObjectDTO (root object container)
    /// - GeometryBuilder (object-type geometry rules)
    /// - IssueDetector (object-type compliance rules)
    /// - OGMBuilder (object graph modeling)
    /// - SMARTshare / SMARTcast (broadcast rules)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - This enum is stable. Values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - This enum represents the *object category*, not the shape subtype.
    /// - ShapeTypeENUM provides the second-level classification for shapes.
    /// </summary>
    public enum ObjectTypeENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Unknown or unclassified object type.
        /// Used when PowerPoint does not expose a specific type.
        /// </summary>
        Unknown = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Shape object (may contain text, geometry, or placeholders).
        /// </summary>
        Shape = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Image or picture object.
        /// </summary>
        Image = 2,

        /// <summary>
        /// Audio media object.
        /// </summary>
        Audio = 3,

        /// <summary>
        /// Video media object.
        /// </summary>
        Video = 4,

        /// <summary>
        /// Table object (structured rows/columns).
        /// </summary>
        Table = 5,

        /// <summary>
        /// Chart object (PowerPoint chart container).
        /// </summary>
        Chart = 6,

        /// <summary>
        /// SmartArt object (diagram container).
        /// </summary>
        SmartArt = 7,

        /// <summary>
        /// Group object (contains multiple child objects).
        /// </summary>
        Group = 8
    }
}

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// TextAlignmentENUM defines the horizontal alignment of text inside a
    /// PowerPoint shape or placeholder. SMARTtools normalizes alignment values
    /// so they can be used consistently across geometry, compliance, and
    /// broadcast rendering.
    ///
    /// Consumed by:
    /// - TextDTO (text metadata container)
    /// - ShapeDTO (shape-level text alignment)
    /// - GeometryBuilder (alignment-aware bounding box adjustments)
    /// - IssueDetector (alignment compliance rules)
    /// - SMARTshare / SMARTcast (broadcast text rendering)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - Stable contract: values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - Represents horizontal alignment only; vertical alignment is handled
    ///   separately by VerticalAlignmentENUM (if you choose to add one).
    /// </summary>
    public enum TextAlignmentENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Text is aligned to the left edge of the text box.
        /// </summary>
        Left = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Text is centered horizontally within the text box.
        /// </summary>
        Center = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Text is aligned to the right edge of the text box.
        /// </summary>
        Right = 2,

        /// <summary>
        /// Text is spaced so that both left and right edges are aligned.
        /// </summary>
        Justify = 3
    }
}

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// TransitionTypeENUM defines the high?level classification of slide
    /// transitions in PowerPoint. SMARTtools normalizes transitions into a
    /// simplified set of categories used across diagnostics, broadcast rules,
    /// and transition geometry.
    ///
    /// Consumed by:
    /// - TransitionDTO (transition metadata container)
    /// - SMARTcast (broadcast transition behavior)
    /// - SMARTshare (export fidelity)
    /// - IssueDetector (transition compliance rules)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - Stable contract: values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - Represents the *type* of transition, not the specific effect variant.
    /// - Specific PowerPoint effects (Fade Smoothly, Wipe Right, Push Up, etc.)
    ///   are normalized into these categories.
    /// </summary>
    public enum TransitionTypeENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// No transition applied. Slide cuts directly to the next slide.
        /// </summary>
        None = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Fade transition (slide dissolves in/out).
        /// </summary>
        Fade = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Wipe transition (slide reveals from a direction).
        /// </summary>
        Wipe = 2,

        /// <summary>
        /// Push transition (new slide pushes the old slide off screen).
        /// </summary>
        Push = 3,

        /// <summary>
        /// Morph transition (PowerPoint morphing engine).
        /// Used for object?aware transitions and continuity animations.
        /// </summary>
        Morph = 4
    }
}

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Enums
{
    /// <summary>
    /// WorkflowStateENUM defines the lifecycle state of an object, document,
    /// or approval packet inside SMARTtools enterprise workflows.
    ///
    /// Consumed by:
    /// - WorkflowDTO (workflow metadata container)
    /// - SMARTmanage (approval routing + governance)
    /// - SMARTshare (distribution gating based on approval state)
    /// - SMARTcast (broadcast restrictions)
    /// - IssueDetector (state?based compliance rules)
    ///
    /// IMPORTANT:
    /// - Stable contract: values may be added, but never removed or renamed.
    /// - Represents the *workflow state*, not the approval decision details.
    /// - Approval metadata lives in ApprovalDTO / ApprovalHistoryDTO.
    /// </summary>
    public enum WorkflowStateENUM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Initial state. Object is being authored or modified.
        /// Not yet submitted for review.
        /// </summary>
        Draft = 0,

        /// <summary>
        /// Object has been submitted and is currently under review.
        /// Multiple approvers may be involved depending on enterprise policy.
        /// </summary>
        Review = 1,

        /// <summary>
        /// Object has passed all required approvals.
        /// Eligible for distribution, broadcast, or publication.
        /// </summary>
        Approved = 2,

        /// <summary>
        /// Object failed approval or was explicitly rejected.
        /// Must return to Draft or be corrected before resubmission.
        /// </summary>
        Rejected = 3
    }
}

using System;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Exceptions
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Thrown when SMARTtools fails to extract context from PowerPoint.
    /// This typically indicates a COM failure, missing slide data,
    /// or an unexpected structure in the host application.
    /// </summary>
    public class ContextExtractionException : Exception
    {
        public ContextExtractionException() { }

        public ContextExtractionException(string message)
            : base(message) { }

        public ContextExtractionException(string message, Exception inner)
            : base(message, inner) { }
    }
}

FOLDER: Core\Extraction\Context

LAST MODIFIED: 2026-07-23 07:53:29

using System.Collections.Generic;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Context
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ContextImages represents the raw image-level context extracted from
    /// a PowerPoint presentation. This DTO is the top-level container for:
    ///
    /// - All images on all slides
    /// - Raw image metadata
    /// - Raw image geometry
    /// - Image resolution and DPI
    /// - Embedded vs. linked image state
    /// - Missing image detection
    ///
    /// ContextImages contains ONLY raw extraction results.
    /// No interpretation, no normalization, no issue detection.
    ///
    /// The Engine layer consumes ContextImages and produces:
    /// - Image geometry models
    /// - Image metadata models
    /// - OGM visual footprint
    /// - Issue lists (resolution, stretching, missing assets)
    /// - Server payloads
    ///
    /// ContextImages is intentionally minimal and stable — it should not change
    /// unless the PowerPoint image extraction model itself changes.
    /// </summary>
    public class ContextImages
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw image metadata entries.
        /// Each entry represents one image extracted from the presentation.
        /// </summary>
        public List<ImageData> Images { get; set; }
            = new List<ImageData>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw geometry footprints for ALL images.
        /// Geometry is stored separately from metadata for clarity.
        /// </summary>
        public List<ImageGeometryData> Geometry { get; set; }
            = new List<ImageGeometryData>();
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// ImageData represents the raw metadata of a single image extracted
    /// from a PowerPoint slide.
    ///
    /// This includes:
    /// - Embedded vs. linked state
    /// - File name and extension
    /// - Resolution (width/height in pixels)
    /// - DPI (horizontal/vertical)
    /// - Missing image detection
    /// - Raw OOXML
    ///
    /// No geometry is stored here — geometry is handled by ImageGeometryData.
    /// </summary>
    public class ImageData
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// PowerPoint's internal image ID.
        /// This is stable and used for mapping across the pipeline.
        /// </summary>
        public int ImageId { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// The slide ID this image belongs to.
        /// </summary>
        public int SlideId { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the image is embedded inside the PPTX file.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsEmbedded { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the image is linked to an external file.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsLinked { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// The file path or URL for linked images.
        /// Empty for embedded images.
        /// </summary>
        public string LinkedPathOrUrl { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if PowerPoint reports the image file as missing.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsMissing { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw pixel width of the image.
        /// </summary>
        public int PixelWidth { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw pixel height of the image.
        /// </summary>
        public int PixelHeight { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Horizontal DPI (dots per inch).
        /// </summary>
        public double DpiX { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Vertical DPI (dots per inch).
        /// </summary>
        public double DpiY { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// File extension (jpg, png, gif, bmp, etc.).
        /// </summary>
        public string FileExtension { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw OOXML for the image element.
        /// Extremely useful for debugging and metadata extraction.
        /// </summary>
        public string RawOOXML { get; set; }
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// ImageGeometryData represents the raw geometric footprint of a single image.
    /// This includes:
    /// - Position (X/Y)
    /// - Size (Width/Height)
    /// - Rotation
    /// - Off-slide detection
    /// - Flipping
    ///
    /// No metadata is stored here — metadata is handled by ImageData.
    /// </summary>
    public class ImageGeometryData
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// PowerPoint's internal image ID.
        /// Links geometry directly to ImageData.
        /// </summary>
        public int ImageId { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw position (Left) in EMUs.
        /// </summary>
        public long X_EMU { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw position (Top) in EMUs.
        /// </summary>
        public long Y_EMU { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw width in EMUs.
        /// </summary>
        public long Width_EMU { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw height in EMUs.
        /// </summary>
        public long Height_EMU { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Rotation in degrees (0–360).
        /// </summary>
        public double RotationDegrees { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the image is positioned outside the visible slide bounds.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsOffSlide { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Distance from the slide center (in EMUs).
        /// </summary>
        public long DistanceFromSlideCenter_EMU { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the image is flipped horizontally.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsFlippedHorizontal { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the image is flipped vertically.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsFlippedVertical { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw OOXML for the image geometry element.
        /// </summary>
        public string RawOOXML { get; set; }
    }
}

using System.Collections.Generic;
using SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Media;
using SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Data;
using SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Geometry;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.MetaData
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ContextMedia represents the raw media-level context extracted from
    /// a PowerPoint presentation. This DTO is the top-level container for:
    ///
    /// - All media objects (video/audio) on all slides
    /// - Raw media metadata (embedded/linked, missing, duration, audio track)
    /// - Raw media geometry (position, size, rotation, off-slide)
    ///
    /// ContextMedia contains ONLY raw extraction results.
    /// No interpretation, no normalization, no issue detection.
    ///
    /// The Engine layer consumes ContextMedia and produces:
    /// - MediaGeometry models
    /// - MediaMetadata models
    /// - OGM media footprint
    /// - Issue lists (missing media, off-slide media, silent videos)
    /// - Server payloads
    ///
    /// ContextMedia is intentionally minimal and stable — it should not change
    /// unless the PowerPoint media extraction model itself changes.
    /// </summary>
    public class ContextMedia
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw media metadata entries.
        /// Each entry represents one media object extracted from the presentation.
        /// </summary>
        public List<MediaData> Media { get; set; }
            = new List<MediaData>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw geometry footprints for ALL media objects.
        /// Geometry is stored separately from metadata for clarity.
        /// </summary>
        public List<MediaGeometryData> Geometry { get; set; }
            = new List<MediaGeometryData>();
    }
}

using System.Collections.Generic;
using SMARTtools.Core.Pipeline;
using SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Slide;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Context
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ====================================================================================================
    /// CONTEXT SLIDES (DTO Container)
    /// ====================================================================================================
    /// PURPOSE:
    ///     Holds ONLY slide-level metadata extracted from PowerPointContext.
    ///
    /// WHY THIS EXISTS:
    ///     - SlideDTO represents *slide metadata only*.
    ///     - Shapes are decomposed into TextDTO, MediaDTO, GeometryDTO, ObjectDTO, etc.
    ///     - IssueDetector and ServerPayloadBuilder expect SlideDTO to be lightweight.
    ///
    /// DESIGN RULE:
    ///     DO NOT store shapes here.
    ///     DO NOT reference ShapeData (it does not exist in your DTO layer).
    ///
    /// PIPELINE:
    ///     PowerPointInteropWrapper ? PowerPointContext.RawSlides ? ContextSlidesBuilder ? EngineContext.Slides
    /// ====================================================================================================
    /// </summary>
    public class ContextSlides
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// List of SlideDTO objects representing slide-level metadata.
        /// </summary>
        public List<SlideDTO> Slides { get; set; }
            = new List<SlideDTO>();

        // ====================================================================================================
        // IMPORTANT:
        //     Shapes are NOT stored here.
        //     They are extracted by:
        //         - ContextTextShapesBuilder (TextDTO)
        //         - ContextMediaBuilder (MediaDTO)
        //         - GeometryBuilder (GeometryDTO)
        //         - PlaceholderGeometryBuilder
        //         - ObjectDTO extraction
        //         - SmartArtDTO extraction
        //         - TableDTO extraction
        //
        //     This keeps ContextSlides clean, stable, and aligned with your DTO folder.
        // ====================================================================================================
    }
}

using System.Collections.Generic;
using SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Data;        // TextData
using SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Geometry;    // GeometryData
using SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Metadata;    // PlaceholderMetadataData

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Context
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ContextTextShapes represents the raw text-shape context extracted from
    /// a PowerPoint presentation. This DTO is the top-level container for:
    ///
    /// - All text-bearing shapes on all slides
    /// - Raw text content (TextData)
    /// - Raw geometry footprint (GeometryData)
    /// - Raw placeholder metadata (if applicable)
    ///
    /// This DTO contains ONLY raw extraction results.
    /// No normalization, no cleanup, no semantic interpretation.
    ///
    /// The Engine layer consumes ContextTextShapes and produces:
    /// - Normalized text models
    /// - Paragraph models
    /// - OGM text footprint
    /// - Issue lists (mixed fonts, inconsistent spacing, empty text)
    /// - Server payloads
    ///
    /// ContextTextShapes is intentionally minimal and stable — it should not change
    /// unless the PowerPoint text extraction model itself changes.
    /// </summary>
    public class ContextTextShapes
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw text data entries.
        /// Each entry represents one text-bearing shape extracted from the presentation.
        /// </summary>
        public List<TextData> TextItems { get; set; }
            = new List<TextData>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of raw geometry footprints for ALL text shapes.
        /// Geometry is stored separately from text content for clarity.
        /// </summary>
        public List<GeometryData> Geometry { get; set; }
            = new List<GeometryData>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Collection of placeholder metadata for text shapes that are placeholders.
        /// This allows the Engine to understand semantic meaning (Title, Body, etc.).
        /// </summary>
        public List<PlaceholderMetadataData> PlaceholderMetadata { get; set; }
            = new List<PlaceholderMetadataData>();
    }
}

using System;
using SMARTtools.Core.Enums;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Extraction.Data
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents raw media data (images, videos, audio) extracted from PowerPoint.
    /// 
    /// ROLE IN ARCHITECTURE:
    /// This is a RAW EXTRACTION MODEL for media objects embedded in presentations.
    /// It captures media exactly as it exists in PowerPoint before optimization.
    /// 
    /// WHY THIS EXISTS:
    /// Media is a major challenge for device optimization:
    /// - Images may be too large (bloat file size)
    /// - Videos may use unsupported codecs (WMV doesn't work on Mac)
    /// - Audio may not play on all devices
    /// - Resolution may be wrong for target device (Retina vs standard)
    /// 
    /// DATA FLOW:
    /// PowerPoint Shape (Picture/Media) ? MediaData ? MediaMapper ? MediaDTO
    /// ? SMARTshare optimizes ? Device-specific media
    /// 
    /// USED BY:
    /// - PowerPointInteropWrapper (populates this)
    /// - MediaGeometryBuilder (extracts media positioning)
    /// - MediaMapper (maps to MediaDTO)
    /// - SMARTshare (optimizes media for target devices)
    /// 
    /// CRITICAL FOR DEVICE OPTIMIZATION:
    /// Media files can be 90% of presentation file size. Device optimization
    /// MUST handle media compression, format conversion, and resolution scaling.
    /// </summary>
    public class MediaData
    {
        // =====================================================================
        // IDENTITY & LOCATION
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the shape ID that contains this media.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THIS:
        /// Media is embedded in shapes. We need to cross-reference back to
        /// ShapeData for geometry calculations (position, size, rotation).
        /// </summary>
        public int ShapeId { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the 1-based slide index this media appears on.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE STORE THIS:
        /// Diagnostics: "Large image on slide 8 will cause slow loading".
        /// Device optimization: "Video on slide 12 needs H.264 conversion".
        /// </summary>
        public int SlideIndex { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets a unique identifier for this media object.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED A MEDIA ID:
        /// Multiple shapes may reference the same embedded image/video.
        /// MediaId allows us to deduplicate media during extraction and optimization.
        /// 
        /// HOW IT'S GENERATED:
        /// Hash of media file content (SHA256). Same image = same MediaId.
        /// </summary>
        public string MediaId { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // MEDIA TYPE CLASSIFICATION
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the media type (image, video, audio).
        /// 
        /// WHY ENUM CLASSIFICATION:
        /// Different media types require different optimization strategies:
        /// - Images: Compression, resolution scaling, format conversion
        /// - Videos: Codec conversion, bitrate adjustment, frame rate
        /// - Audio: Codec conversion, bitrate adjustment, stereo vs mono
        /// </summary>
        public MediaTypeENUM MediaType { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // FILE PROPERTIES
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the original filename (if available from PowerPoint).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THIS:
        /// - Diagnostics can reference "logo.png" instead of "Media object 42"
        /// - Brand compliance may validate logo filenames
        /// - File extension hints at format (logo.png vs logo.jpg)
        /// 
        /// POWERPOINT QUIRK:
        /// Embedded media may not have original filename.
        /// PowerPoint might return "Picture 1" or null.
        /// </summary>
        public string FileName { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the file format/extension (e.g., "png", "jpg", "mp4", "wmv").
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS IS CRITICAL:
        /// Format determines device compatibility:
        /// - PNG/JPG: Universal
        /// - WMV: Windows-only (must convert to MP4 for Mac/iOS)
        /// - MOV: Mac-native (may need conversion for Windows)
        /// - GIF: Animated images (may cause performance issues)
        /// </summary>
        public string FileFormat { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the file size in bytes (embedded media size).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE TRACK SIZE:
        /// Large media causes:
        /// - Slow presentation loading
        /// - Email attachment size limits exceeded
        /// - Network bandwidth issues during download
        /// 
        /// Device optimization uses file size to prioritize compression.
        /// Files >1MB are candidates for aggressive compression.
        /// </summary>
        public long FileSizeBytes { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // IMAGE-SPECIFIC PROPERTIES
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the original image width in pixels (for images only).
        /// 
        /// WHY ORIGINAL WIDTH MATTERS:
        /// If embedded image is 4000px wide but displayed at 500px on slide,
        /// device optimization can downsample to 1000px (2x for Retina) and
        /// save significant file size without quality loss.
        /// </summary>
        public int? OriginalWidthPixels { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the original image height in pixels (for images only).
        /// 
        /// WHY ORIGINAL HEIGHT MATTERS:
        /// Same logic as OriginalWidthPixels. Allows intelligent downsampling.
        /// </summary>
        public int? OriginalHeightPixels { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the DPI (dots per inch) of the embedded image.
        /// 
        /// WHY DPI MATTERS:
        /// Standard screens: 96 DPI
        /// Retina/HiDPI screens: 192+ DPI
        /// 
        /// Device optimization uses DPI to determine if image needs upsampling
        /// (standard ? Retina) or downsampling (Retina ? standard) for target device.
        /// 
        /// POWERPOINT QUIRK:
        /// PowerPoint may report incorrect DPI for imported images.
        /// We validate against actual pixel dimensions.
        /// </summary>
        public int? DPI { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether the image has transparency (alpha channel).
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// - PNG with transparency: Must use PNG on target device (not JPG)
        /// - Transparent images: Cannot use aggressive JPG compression
        /// - Background images: May need special handling for different aspect ratios
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasTransparency { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether the image is cropped in PowerPoint.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE TRACK CROPPING:
        /// Cropped images still contain full image data (wasted space).
        /// Device optimization can crop the actual image file to match visible area.
        /// 
        /// EXAMPLE:
        /// 4000x3000 image cropped to show only 1000x750 region.
        /// Optimization saves 93% of image data by cropping the file itself.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsCropped { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // VIDEO-SPECIFIC PROPERTIES
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the video duration in seconds (for videos only).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED DURATION:
        /// Long videos bloat file size. May need trimming or compression.
        /// Accessibility: Videos >2 minutes may require closed captions.
        /// </summary>
        public double? DurationSeconds { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the video codec (e.g., "H.264", "WMV", "VP9").
        /// 
        /// WHY CODEC IS CRITICAL:
        /// Codec determines device compatibility:
        /// - H.264: Universal (Windows, Mac, iOS, Android)
        /// - WMV: Windows-only (must convert for Mac/iOS)
        /// - VP9/AV1: Modern browsers, not PowerPoint native
        /// 
        /// Device optimization converts WMV/MOV to H.264 for universal compatibility.
        /// </summary>
        public string VideoCodec { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the video resolution (e.g., "1920x1080", "1280x720").
        /// 
        /// WHY RESOLUTION MATTERS:
        /// 4K video (3840x2160) embedded in slide displayed at 640x480 wastes space.
        /// Device optimization downsamples to appropriate resolution for target device.
        /// </summary>
        public string VideoResolution { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the video bitrate in Kbps (kilobits per second).
        /// 
        /// WHY BITRATE MATTERS:
        /// High bitrate = large file size.
        /// Video streaming (SMARTcast) requires bitrate optimization for network bandwidth.
        /// 
        /// TYPICAL BITRATES:
        /// - 480p: 500-1000 Kbps
        /// - 720p: 1500-2500 Kbps
        /// - 1080p: 3000-5000 Kbps
        /// - 4K: 10000+ Kbps
        /// </summary>
        public int? VideoBitrateKbps { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the video frame rate (frames per second).
        /// 
        /// WHY FRAME RATE MATTERS:
        /// 60fps video is larger than 30fps with no quality benefit for presentations.
        /// Device optimization can downsample frame rate to reduce file size.
        /// </summary>
        public int? VideoFrameRate { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // AUDIO-SPECIFIC PROPERTIES
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the audio codec (e.g., "AAC", "MP3", "WMA").
        /// 
        /// WHY CODEC MATTERS:
        /// Similar to video - some codecs are platform-specific.
        /// AAC/MP3 are universal. WMA is Windows-specific.
        /// </summary>
        public string AudioCodec { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the audio bitrate in Kbps.
        /// 
        /// WHY BITRATE MATTERS:
        /// Voice-over narration: 64-96 Kbps sufficient
        /// Music: 128-192 Kbps recommended
        /// 
        /// Device optimization uses bitrate to balance quality vs file size.
        /// </summary>
        public int? AudioBitrateKbps { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether audio is stereo (true) or mono (false).
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// Mono audio is half the size of stereo with no quality loss for voice-over.
        /// Device optimization converts stereo voice-over to mono to save space.
        /// </summary>
        public bool? IsStereo { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // EMBEDDING & LINKING
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether the media is embedded in the presentation file.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED TO KNOW:
        /// Embedded: Media is inside .pptx file (we can optimize it)
        /// Linked: Media is external file path (may not exist on target device)
        /// 
        /// Device optimization can only process embedded media.
        /// Linked media requires special handling (may need to embed for distribution).
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsEmbedded { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the external file path (if media is linked, not embedded).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THE PATH:
        /// Linked media may be:
        /// - Local path: "C:\Images\logo.png" (won't work on recipient device)
        /// - Network path: "\\server\share\video.mp4" (recipient may not have access)
        /// - URL: "https://example.com/video.mp4" (may require internet)
        /// 
        /// Device optimization flags linked media as potential distribution issue.
        /// SMARTshare may offer to embed linked media before sharing.
        /// </summary>
        public string LinkedPath { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // ACCESSIBILITY
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the alt text (alternative text) for this media.
        /// 
        /// WHY ALT TEXT IS CRITICAL:
        /// Accessibility compliance (WCAG, Section 508) REQUIRES alt text on images.
        /// Screen readers cannot describe images without alt text.
        /// 
        /// SMARTcomply validates that all images have descriptive alt text.
        /// 
        /// POWERPOINT QUIRK:
        /// Users frequently forget alt text. This is one of the most common
        /// compliance violations we'll detect.
        /// </summary>
        public string AltText { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether alt text is present.
        /// 
        /// WHY BOOLEAN FLAG:
        /// Faster to check flag than parse AltText string during diagnostics.
        /// We set this during extraction to optimize IssueDetector performance.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasAltText { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // METADATA
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the media extraction timestamp.
        /// 
        /// WHY TIMESTAMP:
        /// Useful for debugging slow extraction (media extraction can be expensive).
        /// Audit trail for when media was captured from presentation.
        /// </summary>
        public DateTime ExtractedAt { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // CONSTRUCTOR
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Initializes a new instance of the <see cref="MediaData"/> class.
        /// 
        /// WHY DEFAULT CONSTRUCTOR:
        /// Extraction code populates properties incrementally as media is
        /// extracted from PowerPoint shapes.
        /// </summary>
        public MediaData()
        {
            // Set extraction timestamp immediately
            ExtractedAt = DateTime.UtcNow;
        }
    }
}

using System.Collections.Generic;
using SMARTtools.Engine;   // Required for NormalizedText
using SMARTtools.Core.DTO;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.DTO.Data
{
    /// <summary>
    /// ============================================================================
    /// OBJECT GEOMETRY MODEL (OGM)
    /// ============================================================================
    /// OGM is the Engine’s unified, fully interpreted representation of a single
    /// PowerPoint slide. It merges geometry, placeholder metadata, media, animations,
    /// and normalized text into one coherent model.
    ///
    /// WHY THIS MODULE EXISTS:
    /// -----------------------
    /// PowerPoint’s raw object model is fragmented:
    ///   - Shapes live in one collection
    ///   - Geometry lives in another
    ///   - Placeholder metadata lives elsewhere
    ///   - Text is extracted separately
    ///   - Animations are stored in yet another subsystem
    ///
    /// OGM consolidates all of these into a single, Engine-ready structure.
    ///
    /// WHAT THIS MODULE DOES:
    /// ----------------------
    /// - Represents a slide as a unified model (OGM)
    /// - Represents each shape as a merged geometry + text + metadata object (OGMShape)
    /// - Represents each media object as a merged geometry + metadata object (OGMMedia)
    /// - Represents animations as normalized geometry footprints (OGMAnimation)
    /// - Stores slide-level issues detected by IssueDetector
    ///
    /// WHAT THIS MODULE DOES NOT DO:
    /// -----------------------------
    /// - It does NOT perform geometry extraction (GeometryBuilder does that)
    /// - It does NOT perform text normalization (TextNormalization does that)
    /// - It does NOT detect issues (IssueDetector does that)
    /// - It does NOT interpret PowerPoint OOXML directly
    ///
    /// PIPELINE ROLE:
    /// --------------
    /// Extraction ? ContextSlides / ContextTextShapes  
    /// Geometry ? GeometryData / PlaceholderGeometryData  
    /// Text ? NormalizedText  
    /// Merge ? OGMBuilder ? OGM  
    /// Consume ? IssueDetector / SMARTshare / SMARTlayout / SMARTwriter
    ///
    /// FUTURE EXPANSION:
    /// -----------------
    /// OGM is intentionally stable and extensible. Future modules may add:
    ///   - semantic layout zones
    ///   - AI-driven shape grouping
    ///   - accessibility scoring
    ///   - animation sequencing models
    ///   - slide-level semantic tagging
    ///
    /// ============================================================================
    /// </summary>
    public class OGM
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Slide index this OGM represents (1-based).
        /// Mirrors SlideData.Index.
        /// </summary>
        public int SlideIndex { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Unified shape models for this slide.
        /// Each OGMShape merges:
        ///   - ShapeData
        ///   - GeometryData
        ///   - PlaceholderGeometryData (if applicable)
        ///   - PlaceholderMetadataData (if applicable)
        ///   - NormalizedText (if applicable)
        ///   - AnimationGeometryData (if applicable)
        ///
        /// This is the Engine’s authoritative shape representation.
        /// </summary>
        public List<OGMShape> Shapes { get; set; }
            = new List<OGMShape>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Unified media models for this slide.
        /// Each OGMMedia merges:
        ///   - MediaData
        ///   - MediaGeometryData
        ///   - AnimationGeometryData (if applicable)
        ///
        /// Media objects are treated separately from shapes because they have
        /// unique geometry and behavioral rules.
        /// </summary>
        public List<OGMMedia> Media { get; set; }
            = new List<OGMMedia>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Unified animation models for this slide.
        /// These represent normalized, geometry-aware animations.
        /// </summary>
        public List<OGMAnimation> Animations { get; set; }
            = new List<OGMAnimation>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Slide-level issues detected by IssueDetector.
        /// These include:
        ///   - empty text
        ///   - overlapping geometry
        ///   - inconsistent placeholder usage
        ///   - layout violations
        ///   - future governance rules
        /// </summary>
        public List<Issue> Issues { get; set; }
            = new List<Issue>();
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// ============================================================================
    /// OGMShape
    /// ============================================================================
    /// Represents a fully interpreted shape on a slide.
    ///
    /// WHAT THIS MERGES:
    /// -----------------
    /// - Shape identity (ShapeId)
    /// - GeometryData (position, size, rotation)
    /// - PlaceholderGeometryData (if the shape is a placeholder)
    /// - PlaceholderMetadataData (semantic meaning: Title, Body, etc.)
    /// - NormalizedText (cleaned text + paragraphs + metrics)
    /// - AnimationGeometryData (animations applied to this shape)
    ///
    /// This is the Engine’s canonical shape representation.
    /// ============================================================================
    /// </summary>
    public class OGMShape
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Unique shape ID (from PowerPoint).
        /// </summary>
        public int ShapeId { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Raw geometry footprint for this shape.
        /// </summary>
        public GeometryData Geometry { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Placeholder geometry footprint (if applicable).
        /// </summary>
        public PlaceholderGeometryData PlaceholderGeometry { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Placeholder semantic metadata (if applicable).
        /// </summary>
        public PlaceholderMetadataData PlaceholderMetadata { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Normalized text associated with this shape (if it is text-bearing).
        /// This is the cleaned, structured, bullet-aware text model.
        /// </summary>
        public NormalizedText NormalizedText { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Geometry-aware animations applied to this shape.
        /// </summary>
        public List<AnimationGeometryData> Animations { get; set; }
            = new List<AnimationGeometryData>();
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// ============================================================================
    /// OGMMedia
    /// ============================================================================
    /// Represents a fully interpreted media object on a slide.
    ///
    /// WHAT THIS MERGES:
    /// -----------------
    /// - MediaData (metadata)
    /// - MediaGeometryData (position, size)
    /// - AnimationGeometryData (animations applied to the media)
    ///
    /// Media objects are treated separately because they have unique geometry
    /// and behavioral rules compared to shapes.
    /// ============================================================================
    /// </summary>
    public class OGMMedia
    {
        public int MediaId { get; set; }

        public MediaData Metadata { get; set; }

        public MediaGeometryData Geometry { get; set; }

        public List<AnimationGeometryData> Animations { get; set; }
            = new List<AnimationGeometryData>();
    }

    /// <summary>
    /// ============================================================================
    /// OGMAnimation
    /// ============================================================================
    /// Represents a fully interpreted animation on a slide.
    ///
    /// WHAT THIS CONTAINS:
    /// -------------------
    /// - AnimationId (identity)
    /// - Geometry footprint (AnimationGeometryData)
    ///
    /// This is the Engine’s normalized representation of animations.
    /// ============================================================================
    /// </summary>
    public class OGMAnimation
    {
        public int AnimationId { get; set; }

        public AnimationGeometryData Geometry { get; set; }
    }
}

using SMARTtools.Core.DTO;
using SMARTtools.Core.Enums;
using SMARTtools.Core.StructuralDTO;
using System;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Extraction
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents raw shape-level data extracted from PowerPoint.
    ///
    /// ROLE IN ARCHITECTURE:
    /// This is a RAW EXTRACTION MODEL for shape geometry and properties.
    /// It holds data exactly as extracted from PowerPoint Interop.
    ///
    /// WHY THIS EXISTS:
    /// Shapes are the fundamental building blocks of presentations.
    /// Everything visible on a slide (text boxes, images, charts, tables, etc.)
    /// is a shape in PowerPoint's object model.
    ///
    /// DATA FLOW:
    /// PowerPoint Shape Object ? ShapeData ? GeometryBuilder ? ShapeMapper ? ShapeDTO
    ///
    /// USED BY:
    /// - PowerPointInteropWrapper (populates identity/classification)
    /// - GeometryBuilder (populates geometry/bounding box)
    /// - ShapeMapper (maps to ShapeDTO)
    ///
    /// CRITICAL FOR DEVICE OPTIMIZATION:
    /// This geometry data is the foundation for SMARTshare's device-adaptive
    /// rendering. If we don't extract positions/sizes accurately, device
    /// optimization fails.
    /// </summary>
    public class ShapeData
    {
        // =====================================================================
        // IDENTITY
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// PowerPoint's internal shape identifier.
        /// Stable across extraction ? mapping ? normalization.
        /// </summary>
        public int Id { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// User-assigned or auto-generated shape name.
        /// Useful for diagnostics and automation.
        /// </summary>
        public string Name { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// 1-based slide index this shape belongs to.
        /// GeometryBuilder uses this to anchor geometry to slide context.
        /// </summary>
        public int SlideIndex { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // SHAPE TYPE & CLASSIFICATION
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Normalized shape type (TextBox, Picture, Placeholder, Media, etc.).
        /// </summary>
        public ShapeTypeENUM ShapeType { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// True if this shape is a placeholder (title, body, footer, etc.).
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsPlaceholder { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Placeholder semantic type (Title, Subtitle, Body, etc.).
        /// </summary>
        public PlaceholderTypeENUM? PlaceholderType { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // GEOMETRY (CRITICAL FOR DEVICE OPTIMIZATION)
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// X coordinate (left edge) in PowerPoint points.
        /// </summary>
        public float Left { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Y coordinate (top edge) in PowerPoint points.
        /// </summary>
        public float Top { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Width in PowerPoint points.
        /// </summary>
        public float Width { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Height in PowerPoint points.
        /// </summary>
        public float Height { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Rotation angle in degrees (0–360).
        /// </summary>
        public float Rotation { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Bounding box (Left/Top/Right/Bottom).
        /// Populated by GeometryBuilder.
        ///
        /// WHY THIS EXISTS:
        /// Overlap detection, off-slide detection, and device optimization
        /// all rely on bounding boxes rather than raw geometry.
        /// </summary>
        public BoundingBoxSDTO BoundingBox { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // CONTENT FLAGS
        // =====================================================================

        public bool HasText { get; set; }
        public bool HasFill { get; set; }
        public bool HasBorder { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // GROUPING
        // =====================================================================

        public bool IsGrouped { get; set; }
        public int? GroupId { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // VISIBILITY & STATE
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// True if the shape is hidden.
        /// Hidden shapes should not be rendered or optimized.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsHidden { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Derived visibility flag.
        /// GeometryBuilder expects IsVisible, but Interop exposes IsHidden.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsVisible => !IsHidden;

        /// <summary>
        /// True if shape is locked and cannot be modified.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsLocked { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // Z-ORDER
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// 1-based Z-order position (layering).
        /// </summary>
        public int ZOrder { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // CONSTRUCTOR
        // =====================================================================

        public ShapeData()
        {
            // Intentionally empty — populated during extraction + geometry build.
        }
    }
}

using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using SMARTtools.Core.Enums;

namespace SMARTtools.Core.Extraction
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Represents raw slide-level data extracted from PowerPoint.
    /// 
    /// ROLE IN ARCHITECTURE:
    /// This is a RAW EXTRACTION MODEL - it holds data exactly as extracted from
    /// PowerPoint Interop, before any normalization or mapping to DTOs occurs.
    /// 
    /// WHY THIS EXISTS:
    /// Separation of concerns: Raw extraction (SlideData) ? Normalization ? 
    /// Mapping (SlideDTO). This keeps the extraction layer simple and the DTO
    /// layer clean.
    /// 
    /// DATA FLOW:
    /// PowerPoint Interop ? SlideData ? SlideMapper ? SlideDTO
    /// 
    /// USED BY:
    /// - PowerPointInteropWrapper (populates this)
    /// - SlideMapper (consumes this to produce SlideDTO)
    /// - ContextSlidesBuilder (aggregates slide context)
    /// 
    /// NOT USED BY PRODUCTS:
    /// Products consume SlideDTO, not SlideData. This is internal platform data.
    /// </summary>
    public class SlideData
    {
        // =====================================================================
        // IDENTITY
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the 1-based slide index (PowerPoint's native indexing).
        /// 
        /// WHY 1-BASED:
        /// PowerPoint uses 1-based indexing (Slide 1, Slide 2, etc.), not 0-based.
        /// We preserve this to avoid confusion when debugging against PowerPoint UI.
        /// </summary>
        public int Index { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the unique slide ID (PowerPoint's internal identifier).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THIS:
        /// SlideIndex can change if slides are reordered. SlideID is stable across
        /// reorders and is required for tracking slides in workflows (SMARTmanage).
        /// </summary>
        public int SlideId { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // LAYOUT & MASTER
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the layout name (e.g., "Title Slide", "Title and Content").
        /// 
        /// WHY WE EXTRACT THIS:
        /// SMARTcomply uses layout information for template enforcement.
        /// SMARTshare uses it to preserve layout structure across devices.
        /// </summary>
        public string LayoutName { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the layout type classification.
        /// 
        /// WHY ENUM INSTEAD OF STRING:
        /// LayoutName can vary ("Title Slide" vs "Title" vs "TitleSlide"), but
        /// LayoutTypeENUM provides consistent classification for validation logic.
        /// </summary>
        public LayoutTypeENUM LayoutType { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the master slide name this slide is based on.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THIS:
        /// Template enforcement (SMARTcomply) requires knowing which master was used.
        /// Device optimization (SMARTshare) may need to reconstruct master relationships.
        /// </summary>
        public string MasterName { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // CONTENT FLAGS
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether this slide has a title placeholder.
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// IssueDetector flags missing titles as a potential issue.
        /// Accessibility compliance (SMARTcomply) requires title placeholders.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasTitlePlaceholder { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether this slide has notes.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE TRACK THIS:
        /// SMARTcast uses notes for speaker view optimization.
        /// SMARTshare may strip notes for distribution (security).
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasNotes { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the notes text (if any).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE EXTRACT THIS:
        /// Copilot integration can generate speaker tips from notes.
        /// SMARTcomply may need to validate notes content for compliance.
        /// </summary>
        public string NotesText { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // BACKGROUND & THEME
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the background type (solid, gradient, picture, etc.).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THIS:
        /// Device optimization (SMARTshare) may need to adjust background rendering
        /// for different screen sizes/resolutions.
        /// </summary>
        public string BackgroundType { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the background fill color (if solid).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE EXTRACT THIS:
        /// Brand compliance (SMARTcomply) validates background colors.
        /// Accessibility checks (SMARTcomply) validate contrast ratios.
        /// </summary>
        public string BackgroundColor { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the theme name applied to this slide.
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// Template enforcement requires theme consistency.
        /// Device optimization may need theme metadata for font substitution.
        /// </summary>
        public string ThemeName { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // TRANSITION & ANIMATION
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether this slide has a transition effect.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE TRACK THIS:
        /// SMARTcast needs to know if transitions are used (may not work on all devices).
        /// Device optimization may need to substitute transitions for compatibility.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasTransition { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the transition type (if any).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE NEED THE TYPE:
        /// Some transitions don't work on Mac or web viewers.
        /// SMARTshare needs to know which transitions to substitute.
        /// </summary>
        public string TransitionType { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether this slide has animations on any objects.
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// Animations complicate device optimization (different engines render differently).
        /// SMARTcast needs to know if animations exist for broadcast optimization.
        /// </summary>
        public bool HasAnimations { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // METADATA
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the slide name (if custom name set by user).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE EXTRACT THIS:
        /// Users sometimes name slides for navigation.
        /// SMARTmanage workflows may reference slides by name.
        /// </summary>
        public string SlideName { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets whether this slide is hidden in slideshow.
        /// 
        /// WHY THIS MATTERS:
        /// Hidden slides may contain sensitive content.
        /// SMARTshare needs to respect hidden status when distributing.
        /// </summary>
        public bool IsHidden { get; set; }

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the creation timestamp (if available from PowerPoint).
        /// 
        /// WHY WE TRACK THIS:
        /// Audit trails (SMARTaudit) require creation timestamps.
        /// Workflow approvals (SMARTmanage) may use timestamps for versioning.
        /// </summary>
        public DateTime? CreatedAt { get; set; }

        // =====================================================================
        // COLLECTIONS
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the list of shape IDs present on this slide.
        /// 
        /// WHY WE STORE SHAPE IDS:
        /// We don't store full shape data here (that's in ShapeData).
        /// We store IDs so we can cross-reference shapes to slides later.
        /// 
        /// DATA MODEL DECISION:
        /// SlideData references ShapeData by ID, not composition.
        /// This avoids duplication and keeps extraction models lightweight.
        /// </summary>
        public List<int> ShapeIds { get; set; } = new List<int>();

        /// <summary>
        /// Gets or sets the list of placeholder IDs on this slide.
        /// 
        /// WHY SEPARATE FROM ShapeIds:
        /// Placeholders are a special kind of shape with semantic meaning
        /// (title, content, footer, etc.). We track them separately for
        /// layout analysis and template enforcement.
        /// </summary>
        public List<int> PlaceholderIds { get; set; } = new List<int>();

        // =====================================================================
        // CONSTRUCTOR
        // =====================================================================

        /// <summary>
        /// Initializes a new instance of the <see cref="SlideData"/> class.
        /// 
        /// WHY DEFAULT CONSTRUCTOR:
        /// Extraction code creates SlideData instances and populates properties
        /// incrementally as data is extracted from PowerPoint Interop.
        /// </summary>
        public SlideData()
        {
            // Intentionally empty - properties populated during extraction
        }
    }
}
